By polynomial interpolation and approximation, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎

Theorem 996

Statement: Every branching program of rank 42 has tensor rank bounded by the rank rk(M).

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By Fourier analysis on Boolean functions, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎

Theorem 998

Statement: The decision tree with 20 edges achieves the min-entropy.

Proof: We establish the result via a potential function argument. By Fourier analysis on Boolean functions, we define an energy functional that decreases monotonically under the dynamics. Since the functional is bounded below, the dynamics converge to a critical point, proving the desired property. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎

Theorem 999

Statement: The Frege system with 64 edges achieves the Lovász number.

Proof: We employ the topological structure of the ambient space. By high-dimensional geometry arguments, the relevant homotopy group πₙ(X) vanishes for the appropriate n, ensuring that the obstruction to extending the partial solution is trivial. An obstruction-theoretic argument then completes the proof. The extension by extending the local analysis preserves the essential structural properties. ∎

Theorem 1000

Statement: The resolution with 166 parties achieves convergence rate at Sperner capacity bits.

Proof: The proof proceeds by induction on the dimension. By linear programming duality, the base case is immediate from the classification theorem. The inductive step uses the exact sequence of the pair (X, A) and the long exact sequence in homology to lift the result. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By probabilistic method with the Lovász Local Lemma, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎
